The goal was not to replace the SDR; it was to make the account universe cleaner and the seller's first judgment better.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | AI is strongest when it turns scattered evidence into a better starting point for human judgment. In prospecting, that means better account selection, stronger signals, cleaner CRM context, and less time wasted researching low-fit accounts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Living feedback loop**                            | The system should improve from sales usage. If sellers reject a row, the reason should update the taxonomy or QA rule. If a high-scoring account is actually a poor fit, the rubric should change. If a duplicate slips through, entity-resolution rules should improve. If a campaign performs well in one subsegment, that feedback should update scoring and routing. CRM enrichment is not a one-time import; it is a living market model.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | A GTM engineering playbook should learn from every row, reply, meeting, rejection, and closed-won or closed-lost pattern. The account universe is not static, and neither should the enrichment system be.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

Salescraft / Uptick metrics drill:

| If they ask | Sharp answer |
|---|---|
| **What exactly did the system do?** | "It combined inbound and CRM records with Google Maps, websites, directories, and scraped website content. Rules removed obvious bad records first. AI then separated real service companies from false positives, classified maintenance versus install work, and helped surface size or location clues. Finally, we deduped branches into parent accounts where the evidence supported it and enriched people and phone numbers for the calling team." |
| **What fields mattered?** | "Account name, working website, country, CRM match status, maintenance / install / both, parent-company and branch evidence, location count, team-size clues, relevant people, main phone number, confidence, source URL, and QA note." |
| **How did scoring work?** | "The documented system produced a 0-100 fit score. Classification carried the largest weight, with support from website quality, maintenance/service focus, services offered, employee count, and contact availability. The important point was not the exact formula; it was that the score mapped to sales routing and QA, not vanity enrichment." |
| **How did you avoid poisoning the CRM?** | "Separate CRM patch records from net-new records, preserve source evidence, confidence-label inferred fields, sample rows before import, suppress weak or duplicate records, and route ambiguous branch/parent cases to manual review." |
| **What was the measurable outcome?** | "I would keep it conservative: improved TAM visibility, better CRM completeness, cleaner account routing, more source-backed account selection, and a higher-confidence sales queue. I would not claim revenue or pipeline lift unless we had a separate validated source." |
| **Why is this relevant to OpenAI sales?** | "It shows the same pattern I would use in Strategic BDR work: do not just write more emails. Build the account intelligence layer: source-of-truth context, signal quality, evidence-backed account selection, CRM hygiene, and clean handoff to the AD." |

90-second version:

> Uptick was a Salescraft project where the customer already had inbound leads, but wanted one clean map of the wider US fire-protection market.
>
> The raw data was messy. Google Maps could return duplicate listings from overlapping searches, businesses outside the target country, broken websites, and companies that looked relevant by name but were actually fire departments or equipment sellers.
>
> We cleaned it in layers. First, simple rules removed obvious bad records. Under the client's rule, a credible company needed a working website, so missing or broken sites were disqualified. Then AI read the scraped website content and judged whether the company really provided fire-protection services.
>
> We also calibrated the ICP with a golden set of about 50 strong companies from the CRM. We watched how reps reviewed websites and used AI to find the common patterns. That helped us realize the strongest ICP was maintenance and recurring service, not installation alone, so we split the market into maintenance, install, and companies that did both.
>
> Finally, we grouped duplicate branches under one parent account where we had enough evidence, enriched the relevant people, and pulled the main phone number for the cold-calling team. Anything uncertain stayed in manual review rather than being pushed into CRM as fact.
>
> The simple lesson is: use rules for the obvious decisions, AI for the messy judgment, and people for the uncertain cases. That gave the reps a cleaner market map and a list they could actually call without filling the CRM with duplicates or false certainty.

Truth boundaries:

- Do say: vertical TAM discovery, CRM enrichment, crawler data, HubSpot enrichment, local discovery, website evidence, entity resolution, 0-100 scoring, QA sampling, CRM-safe activation, and source-backed routing.
- Do say: the workflow used a golden set of about 50 strong CRM accounts and observed how reps reviewed websites to sharpen the ICP toward recurring maintenance and service.
- Do say: obvious exclusions were handled with rules first; AI was used for website-level judgment; ambiguous records stayed in manual review.
- Do say: the outcome was better TAM visibility, CRM quality, account routing, and sales confidence.
- Do not say: autonomous pipeline generation, verified revenue lift, closed-won lift, or perfect entity resolution unless separately proven.
- Do not give a precise nationwide company count or treat the 50-plus-employee threshold as a verified final rule unless separately confirmed.
- Do not overstate inferred branch counts, parent/subsidiary relationships, or service classifications as verified facts without source evidence and confidence labels.

#### Canonical Case Table: Salescraft / Complex Scraping And Technology-Signal Verification

One specialised Salescraft engagement involved identifying highly specific buying signals that normal company databases could not reliably provide. For an ecommerce client, the signal was whether a prospect used a particular payment gateway such as Stripe. For another client competing with human-capital-management platforms, the signal was which recruitment or HCM system a company used. Because the client already integrated with certain platforms, identifying those accounts upfront removed a large amount of qualification and discovery work for their sales team.

In plain language, the script:

- **Opens the company's homepage.**
- **Checks its sitemap and website links** to find likely pages such as Careers, Jobs, Pricing, Cart, or Checkout.
- **Opens those pages in a real Chromium browser** using Playwright.
- **Inspects the rendered HTML** for scripts, links, and embedded iframes.
- **Watches network traffic and redirects**, which can reveal technology that is not written directly in the HTML.
- **Matches the evidence against known fingerprints**, such as:
  - `js.stripe.com` → Stripe
  - `careers-company.icims.com` → iCIMS
  - `jobs.dayforcehcm.com` → Dayforce
  - `myworkdayjobs.com` → Workday
- **Records the result and supporting URL** in the TAM or CRM file.
- **Leaves uncertain cases unconfirmed** for manual review.

- I began with the same TAM workflow: source the initial company list, clean and deduplicate it, enrich the standard company fields, and define exactly what evidence would count as confirmed.

- Public databases were useful for generating hypotheses, but not as final proof. For example, [Store Leads lists GameStop among prominent ecommerce stores using Stripe](https://storeleads.app/reports/technology/Stripe#topstores). However, vendor databases can be stale or wrong, so I treated that as a lead to verify rather than a confirmed result.

- I built and ran the verification workflow entirely on my laptop using Cursor, Playwright, and a local Chromium browser. Cursor helped me write and adjust the scripts, but the actual verification process was deterministic rather than an AI agent guessing its way around the website.

- The browser opened each company's website, discovered likely routes through its navigation and links, and then followed the relevant customer journey. For payment detection, it would move from the homepage or product page into the [GameStop cart](https://www.gamestop.com/cart/) and toward checkout, without entering payment information or completing a purchase.

- During that journey, Playwright inspected redirects, network requests, scripts, and embedded frames. A database claim was not enough: I only marked Stripe as confirmed when the live payment flow exposed direct evidence such as a Stripe-hosted checkout, Stripe network traffic, or a Stripe-controlled payment iframe. If the evidence never appeared, the result remained `not detected` or was sent for manual review.

- The hiring-platform workflow followed the same principle. The browser found the Careers page, opened a vacancy, and followed the application path. In the [HealthPRO Heritage example](https://careers-healthpro-rehab.icims.com/jobs/intro?hashed=-435626184&mobile=false&width=990&height=500&bga=true&needsRedirect=false&jan1offset=-300&jun1offset=-240), the careers portal is hosted directly on an `icims.com` domain. That provides strong, first-party evidence that HealthPRO uses iCIMS for its public recruiting workflow.

- Finally, I wrote the confirmed technology, supporting URL, evidence type, confidence, and verification date back into the enriched TAM or CRM file. The client therefore received more than a list of companies: they received a prioritised account list with evidence explaining why each account matched the ICP.

- The core idea was to use broad data sources for discovery and a real browser for verification. That produced narrower but much more actionable prospect lists, reduced qualification work for sales representatives, and gave the client evidence they could audit rather than asking them to trust a black-box database.

- The truth boundary is high-confidence, auditable evidence from the live customer journey, not a claim of absolute certainty. An empty cart or a third-party database listing alone does not prove which payment provider processes the eventual transaction.

#### Canonical Case Table: Unabank Enterprise Deal At Boxo

Question it answers: "Walk me through a complex enterprise deal," "How do you multi-thread?", "How do you sell a technical product to a regulated buyer?", or "How do you make a young vendor credible?"

Short answer:

> The Unabank story is my best proof that enterprise selling is structured risk removal. Boxo was early, the product was technical, and the buyer was a serious fintech.
>
> At the time, Unabank sat within an international financial group with roughly USD 400 million in annual revenue and more than 3,500 employees, so even though we entered through a specific use case, the opportunity had genuine enterprise scale and complexity.
>
> The sale was not just "do they like the product?" It was: can the CEO defend the business case, can product believe the experience will work, can engineering trust the integration, can legal/compliance underwrite the risk, and can our own team deliver without overpromising?

Source and deployment basis:

- Seth-specific source basis: [career-ops operator apprenticeship](/Users/sethlim/Documents/career-ops/interview-prep/life-history/03-operator-apprenticeship.md), [career-ops enterprise sales playbook](/Users/sethlim/Documents/career-ops/interview-prep/enterprise-sales-playbook.md),
  and the Boxo metrics / deal-card material in this prep vault.
- Second Brain methodology basis: High-Signal Enterprise Sales for champion enablement, buyer co-authorship, proof proximity, and gap selling; Founder-Led Enterprise Sales Playbooks for enterprise deal stages; AI-Native Account Intelligence for proof gaps, champion risk, finance readiness, and AD handoff fields.
- Deployment spine to keep in mind: discovery, use-case scoping, technical validation, stakeholder alignment, executive business case, legal/compliance/risk review, commercial close, implementation handoff, and post-close reference learning.
- Truth boundary: use this as a MEDDICC-style reconstruction, not a claim that Boxo formally ran MEDDICC. Do not hard-claim exact Unabank ARR/ACV basis unless Seth confirms it.

Unabank example and playbook:

| Principle                                           | Unabank / Boxo detail                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Playbook lesson                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| --------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Diagnosis before tactics**                        | Boxo was still early and selling before the enterprise playbook really existed. The company did not have the normal enterprise assets a serious fintech buyer would expect: mature references, long implementation history, polished security collateral, or a repeatable buyer motion. The product was SDK-based miniapp infrastructure: a software layer that let large host apps embed third-party services inside their own app experience. A miniapp is basically a lightweight, native-like web app that opens inside another app. So instead of sending a Unabank user out to a separate merchant website or asking Unabank to build every merchant feature natively, Boxo let Unabank open that merchant journey inside the Unabank app, with controlled handoffs for things like login, payment, user context, theme, and status updates. The contrast was important. A normal browser link was easy to launch, but it broke the app experience and could not connect cleanly back to Unabank's own payment flow. A true native build gave the most control, but every merchant or category would become a real product and engineering release. Even if Unabank used something like Flutter to share much of the iOS and Android codebase, it would still need product scoping, design, backend work, QA across devices, release planning, app-store submission, app-store review, possible rejection or resubmission, rollout, maintenance, and future updates. The app-store review point was painful because launch timing stopped being fully in the team's control. A merchant launch could get trapped behind mobile release cadence instead of moving at partner speed. The miniapp path sat in the middle: web-based and faster to launch, but still in-app enough for Unabank to preserve the user experience and connect the journey to login and payment. That did not mean zero App Store governance. The host app still remained responsible for privacy, consent, payments, and policy compliance. The advantage was narrower: Unabank did not need to ship a new native app release for every merchant launch. It is PWA-like in the sense that the service is web-based, but the important sales point is that it feels like an in-app service, not a random browser redirect. For Unabank, the initial wedge was an ecommerce marketplace inside the fintech app, but the real commercial question was broader: could Boxo help Unabank become more of a super-app-style financial platform with more service categories, more user engagement, and faster launch cycles?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | The first job was not to run a generic enterprise cadence. It was to diagnose the maturity gap: serious buyer, young vendor, technical product, regulated context, and a buyer that needed to underwrite risk internally before it could say yes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **TAM segmentation before ICP**                     | Boxo's ICP was not "any company with an app." The sharper ICP was a superapp candidate: a large consumer platform that was already strong in one core vertical, had roughly 10M+ MAU or enough logged-in distribution for new categories to matter, and owned a wallet or payment relationship. That was the pattern behind the strongest superapps. Grab and Gojek started from high-frequency mobility and logistics use cases, then expanded into food, delivery, payments, merchant services, and financial services. WeChat started from messaging and social, then layered in WeChat Pay and mini programs so users could complete tasks without leaving the app. Kaspi was the financial-services version: bank/payments first, then marketplace, fintech, QR payments, and daily services inside one app. The strategic idea was that once a platform had a core habit, a wallet, and serious MAU, embedded services could increase retention, monetization, partner revenue, and share of attention. The dream state was not users scrolling their phone to find a separate app; it was users opening the trusted superapp surface and getting the service there. Unabank fit because it had a core financial product, consumer distribution, logged-in users, payment-adjacent workflows, product ambition, and enough internal roadmap pressure that a reusable embedded-services layer could matter. The anti-ICP was just as important: a small app with no meaningful audience, no wallet or payment relationship, no strong core vertical, no executive push to expand the app, or no ability to support third-party user journeys would have looked interesting on paper but would not have created the same enterprise value.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | A TAM can look huge if you say "apps," but the winnable ICP was much narrower: platforms with a strong core vertical, roughly 10M+ MAU or meaningful logged-in distribution, a wallet/payment layer, product ambition, and enough internal pressure to launch new services faster than their own roadmap could support. A good playbook should say who not to chase: users alone are not enough; the account needs a habit, a payment relationship, and a workflow where embedded services change engagement, revenue, or strategic positioning.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **Market map before account list**                  | Before I could prospect intelligently, I had to build the universe of accounts. I was not looking for generic mobile apps; I was mapping every bank, digital bank, wallet, exchange, superapp, telco app, and large consumer platform that could plausibly become a superapp-style buyer. Then I grouped them by the core vertical they already owned: finance, payments, mobility, delivery, messaging, ecommerce, crypto, telco, or other high-frequency consumer behavior. To size the universe, I could also scrape public app-store signals: Google Play install bands where available, and iOS proxies like review volume, ratings, category rank, ranking movement, and third-party estimates, since Apple does not expose exact downloads publicly. That gave me a practical way to proxy distribution and separate apps with real consumer reach from apps that only looked good in a spreadsheet. Then I would do a manual product audit: download the app, log in where possible, and see whether it had already launched third-party services, miniapps, partner offers, or marketplace journeys. That was both a good and bad sign. If an app already had third-party services, it could mean the buyer understood the category and had user demand, but it could also mean they had already built an integration layer in-house or were using a white-label platform partner like Alibaba. For example, with large wallets like GCash, DANA, and Touch 'n Go, the discovery question was not just "do they have third-party services?" It was "how are they powering this, are they happy with the platform, and where are the gaps?" If an app had not launched third-party services yet, but there were clear partnership, ecosystem, product, or third-party services stakeholders in the org and they were willing to talk, that was often a strong timing signal that the app was moving in this direction. From there, I filtered for the real Boxo ingredients: meaningful MAU or install base proxy, a logged-in user base, a wallet or payment relationship, executive ambition to expand app surface area, and obvious adjacent categories like commerce, travel, eSIMs, marketplace services, partner offers, or other miniapp journeys. Strictly speaking, anyone outside that ICP was a complete waste of time, no matter how interested they sounded. Active disqualification was paramount because a small app could still consume a huge amount of sales, product, and engineering attention. I learned that early after going deep with one smaller app where there was one eager champion, but no real executive sponsorship, no strategic platform pressure, and no account-level reason to underwrite the work. The market map was useful because it forced me to see which accounts had the right platform shape and which accounts were only superficially interesting.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | A good account list is the output of a market map, not the starting point. For platform sales, the map should show core habit, distribution, wallet/payment layer, category adjacency, product ambition, and internal roadmap pressure. Public app-store data is useful because it gives a proxy for reach before deeper discovery. A manual product audit adds another layer: existing third-party services can signal readiness or competition, while a clear partnerships org without a live surface can signal near-term movement. Active disqualification matters just as much as account discovery: interest is not qualification if the app lacks distribution, executive sponsorship, payment relationship, or strategic pressure. Otherwise it is too easy to chase "apps" that have traffic on paper but no real reason to buy embedded-services infrastructure.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Finite-TAM trust**                                | Boxo's serious fintech/platform buyer universe was finite. If Seth overpromised, mishandled technical concerns, or treated the buyer like a simple logo chase, the damage would not just be one lost opportunity. It would weaken the company's credibility in a small regional ecosystem where references, investors, founders, and platform teams talk.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | In a finite enterprise market, trust is the territory. Especially as an early vendor, every interaction either creates proof or creates doubt. The seller has to preserve credibility even when trying to move quickly.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **Tiering**                                         | Unabank mattered because it was not just another account. It was remembered as Boxo/Appboxo's first real enterprise client and a referenceable proof point. If Boxo could win and implement Unabank well, it would earn the right to have bigger platform conversations later. That made it the sweet spot: ambitious enough to matter, serious enough to have budget and group backing, but still startup-minded enough to take a calculated bet on another startup. It deserved founder-level attention, product involvement, travel, in-person trust-building, and careful scoping because the downstream value was company credibility, investor confidence, future customer proof, and internal team belief.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Tiering should include strategic proof value, not only contract size. Some accounts matter because they create the reference that makes the next ten conversations easier. The best early enterprise logos are often not the biggest possible companies; they are credible buyers that are ambitious, resourced, and willing to co-create.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Right signal, right timing**                      | The signal was not just "Unabank has an app." During that period, BNPL apps were taking off, especially in Southeast Asia, and mobile-first ecommerce was becoming a very clear strategic category. The cleaner trigger was not that Unabank had just fundraised; public sources point to UnaCash being part of UnaFinancial, a Singapore-headquartered fintech group. So the account had a better shape than a small standalone app: group backing, a live financial product, regional ambition, and a strategic reason to extend into marketplace and merchant journeys. That mattered because their target market was in Southeast Asia, where consumer internet behavior is heavily mobile-first. The clean way to support that is not to invent one magic region-wide number: e-Conomy SEA describes the region as mobile-first and estimates roughly 500M internet users, while GSMA's APAC data showed smartphone adoption already at 87% in Indonesia, 86% in the Philippines, and 91% in Singapore in 2023. In other words, if Unabank was building for Southeast Asian consumers, the app was the front door. We reached out straight to the CEO rather than a generic BD inbox, and the outreach connected their marketplace / BNPL roadmap to Boxo's own credibility proof: we had publicly raised a Series A, had recognizable investors and backers, and could point to major wallet / superapp-style logos in the category. If Unabank wanted to hit an ambitious roadmap, it would need support rolling out key merchants that connected to its BNPL payment option, all inside its own app experience. The timing pain was that building every merchant or service integration in-house would slow the roadmap. In that sense, the real competitor was not only another vendor; it was internal build, internal roadmap drag, and the risk that every new category became a bespoke product / engineering / risk / support project. The message was not "buy our SDK." It was "use Boxo to launch the key merchant journeys faster, connect them to your BNPL payment option, and keep the whole experience inside the Unabank app."                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | A signal becomes messageable when category timing, group-backed ambition, roadmap priority, product fit, and credible proof all line up. "You have a mobile app" is weak. "BNPL is taking off, you are building a mobile-first financial product, marketplace journeys are strategically adjacent, and you need merchant journeys connected to your payment option faster than internal build can support" is a real conversation. Going straight to the CEO only works if the note has proof: why this matters now, why we are credible enough to take seriously, and why the account should not treat the outreach as generic vendor noise. For platform sales, internal build is often the real alternative to beat.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **Proof proximity**                                 | Because Boxo was early, I could not walk in and say, "here are five enterprise case studies proving this already works." I had to create confidence during the sales process itself. That meant showing the product, translating it into Unabank's ecommerce marketplace use case, bringing product and engineering into the right conversations, answering questions quickly, and being honest about what was already supported versus what would need custom work. For the buyer, the practical questions were simple: can this sit inside our app, how does the user log in, how does payment work, what data moves where, who owns support if a merchant flow breaks, how do we test this before production, and who do we call if something goes wrong? Every clear answer became a small piece of proof. Later, once Unabank became real, that proof point helped Boxo tell a much more credible story to investors, future customers, and the internal team.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | When external proof is thin, the seller has to create proof through the way they run the process: clear scope, fast answers, honest risk framing, and visible internal follow-through. Proof proximity can come from the buyer feeling, "these people understand our workflow and can actually get this done," not only from a finished logo slide.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **Buyer committee**                                 | The buyer committee was distributed across functions and countries. Seth remembers dealing directly with the CEO in Singapore and the Product Head in the Philippines, while working through engineering feasibility with the engineering team in Ukraine. The CEO cared about strategic upside: expanding what users could do inside the app, creating more engagement, adding revenue opportunities, and launching faster than building internally. The Product Head cared about whether the miniapp marketplace would feel like a real product experience: UX, roadmap fit, reusable launch pattern, and control over brand/user journey. Engineering cared about SDK/API feasibility, authentication, payment/session handoffs, documentation, support ownership, and maintenance burden. Legal/compliance/risk cared about data handling, user consent, vendor maturity, third-party liability, SLAs, and reputational risk because this was a fintech buyer evaluating a young infrastructure vendor. Internally, Boxo product and engineering had to be brought into the loop because customer objections often became product-scoping questions. The product workflow map was part of the stakeholder map: Unabank host app, Boxo SDK layer, third-party merchant/service miniapp, user session, auth handoff, payment or wallet-adjacent flow, support ownership, data boundary, and escalation path if something broke.
